# 17. Edge AI deployment concepts
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Deploy and manage a compact edge inference service
---
## Executive definition
Edge AI places inference near data sources to reduce latency, bandwidth, and privacy exposure, while central platforms govern models, images, configuration, telemetry, and fleet rollout.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Choose edge placement criteria.
- Optimize models for constraints.
- Design disconnected operation.
- Implement fleet rollout and rollback.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Low Latency
- Define what **low latency** means for Edge AI deployment concepts.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for low latency.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for low latency.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for low latency, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for low latency.
- Review how low latency changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: edge OpenShift
- **Purpose:** Describe why edge OpenShift is relevant to Edge AI deployment concepts and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by edge OpenShift.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by edge OpenShift.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by edge OpenShift.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for edge OpenShift.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for edge OpenShift.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for edge OpenShift.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "edge-ai-deployment"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: edge-ai-deployment
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/edge-ai-deployment
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Deploy and manage a compact edge inference service
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **Edge AI deployment concepts**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for edge OpenShift, with explicit consideration of low latency.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for single-node OpenShift, with explicit consideration of limited resources.
3. Create objective entry and exit criteria for accelerator, with explicit consideration of intermittent connectivity.
4. Identify dependencies and support boundaries for quantized model, with explicit consideration of model compression.
5. Capture security and compliance requirements for inference runtime, with explicit consideration of hardware diversity.
6. Define evidence retained for audit for local cache, with explicit consideration of fleet management.
7. Write an automation or gitops approach for GitOps, with explicit consideration of secure updates.
8. Define validation commands and expected results for registry mirror, with explicit consideration of telemetry.
9. Document failure modes and rollback for device data, with explicit consideration of fallback.
10. Review capacity, cost, and licensing impact for telemetry buffer, with explicit consideration of data minimization.
11. Peer-review the design with platform and application teams for central model registry, with explicit consideration of low latency.
12. Obtain change approval before production for rollout controller, with explicit consideration of limited resources.
**Phase evidence:**
- Approved record for business framing and success criteria.
- Configuration or design artifact stored in Git for edge-ai-deployment.
- Test output showing both successful and intentionally failed paths.
- Named owner and escalation path for unresolved risk.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for Edge AI deployment concepts, using edge OpenShift as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate low latency, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate limited resources, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate intermittent connectivity, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate model compression, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate hardware diversity, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate fleet management, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate secure updates, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate telemetry, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The edge OpenShift state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to limited resources.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.

### Lab 3: Create a governed project namespace

## Enterprise control catalogue
### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** Edge AI deployment concepts must implement documented identity and access controls for edge OpenShift.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for intermittent connectivity, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.
- **Review frequency:** Review after material platform changes and on the organization's scheduled control cycle.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for Edge AI deployment concepts.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Edge AI deployment concepts and is first observed around inference runtime.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass limited resources controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Edge AI deployment concepts and is first observed around telemetry buffer.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass intermittent connectivity controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 3: Workbench Remains Pending
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Edge AI deployment concepts and is first observed around accelerator.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Data distribution or quality shift.
- **First command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get storageclass`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass model compression controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 4: Pvc Does Not Bind
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Edge AI deployment concepts and is first observed around registry mirror.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Namespace or project mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get datasciencecluster -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc logs <pod> -n <namespace> --all-containers`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass hardware diversity controls.

- **First command:** `oc get dscinitialization -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc get resourcequota,limitrange -A`

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports Edge AI deployment concepts?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.
### Decision 18: Local Versus Remote Data Access
- **Question:** Which local versus remote data access pattern best supports Edge AI deployment concepts?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate AI platform to edge OpenShift, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 2: MLOps
- **Definition:** Practices that automate and govern the machine-learning lifecycle.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate MLOps to single-node OpenShift, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 3: GenAIOps
- **Definition:** Operational practices for foundation-model, RAG, prompt, agent, evaluation, and inference lifecycles.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate GenAIOps to accelerator, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 4: ModelOps
- **Definition:** Portfolio-wide governance and operations for models, versions, approvals, deployments, and risk.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate ModelOps to quantized model, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 5: Workbench
- **Definition:** A project-scoped interactive development environment such as JupyterLab or code-server.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate Workbench to inference runtime, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 6: Pipeline
- **Definition:** A repeatable workflow of containerized tasks for data and model lifecycle automation.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate Pipeline to local cache, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 7: Model registry
- **Definition:** A governed metadata and artifact catalog for model versions and lifecycle state.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate Model registry to GitOps, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 8: Model catalog
- **Definition:** A curated discovery source for models that can be evaluated, registered, or deployed.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate Model catalog to registry mirror, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 9: Model serving
- **Definition:** Runtime services that expose trained models through network APIs.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate Model serving to device data, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 10: Inference
- **Definition:** Execution of a trained model to produce a prediction or generated output.
- **Relevance:** In Edge AI deployment concepts, relate Inference to telemetry buffer, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 11: RAG
- **Definition:** Retrieval-augmented generation that grounds model responses in retrieved enterprise content.

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **Edge AI deployment concepts** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
